MPI_Type_contiguous(2, MPI_DOUBLE, &dyn_type);\n    MPI_Type_commit(&dyn_type);\n    if (rank == 0) {\n        global.resize(part.n_global);\n        std::copy(local.begin(), local.end(), global.begin());\n        for (int r = 1; r < part.n_ranks; ++r) {\n            const idx_t b = part.begin(r), n = part.end(r) - b;\n            for (idx_t off = 0; off < n; off += CHUNK) {\n                const int cnt = static_cast<int>(std::min<idx_t>(CHUNK, n - off));\n                MPI_Recv(global.data() + b + off, cnt, dyn_type, r, 1, comm, MPI_STATUS_IGNORE);\n            }\n        }\n {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfkjaAikTHfBdZMeugRPM","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The benchmark now runs as an MPI + OpenMP + CUDA program, still built as the `unstructured` executable. Every run I tested gives exactly the same result hashes as the original serial code, for 1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 ranks and mesh sizes from 2×2 to 8192×8192.\n\n**Speed on this node (4× RTX 3090):**\n\n| Run | Serial | 1 GPU | 2 GPUs | 4 GPUs |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 4096², 100 iterations | 15,988 ms | 156 ms | 82 ms | 43 ms |\n| 8192², 100 iterations | — | 567 ms | 299 ms | 163 ms |\n\nThat's about 100× faster than the serial code on one GPU, and close to linear scaling across GPUs (≈3.6× on 4 for 4096², ≈3.5× for 8192²).\n\n**How it's split up (all in `unstructured.cpp`):**\n- **MPI:** the elements are divided into contiguous index ranges, one per rank, and each rank builds only its own part of the mesh. The neighbour data each rank needs from other ranks is worked out from the general connection lists, not from the grid shape, so it still works as an unstructured mesh. These values are exchanged every iteration.\n- **CUDA:** each rank uses one GPU on its node. Elements that need no data from other ranks are updated while that exchange is in progress; the rest are updated once the data arrives. Open MPI here isn't CUDA-aware, so the exchange goes through host memory.\n- **OpenMP:** used for the host-side work: building the mesh, setting up GPU data, reordering results and hashing.\n- **Results:** the hash is computed in parallel and combined across ranks. Results are only collected on rank 0 when `-r` or `-v` is passed, and validation sums in the original order so its printed numbers don't change.\n- **Timing:** still covers only the simulation loop.\n\n**Exact results:**\n- The CUDA build uses `-fmad=false` and keeps the original order of operations, which is why the output matches the CPU code exactly.\n- nvcc 12.0 miscompiled my first version of the kernel: it computed `fabs(total_flux)` from the value before the neighbour loop, so the flux part of the hash was wrong while energies were still correct. I found it by reading the generated GPU assembly and restructured the loop to avoid it. The tests above were all run after that fix.\n\n**`CMakeLists.txt`:** now enables CUDA, MPI and OpenMP and compiles the `.cpp` file with nvcc. The GPU architecture defaults to `native`, so set `CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES` when building on a machine with a different GPU from the one it will run on. No new dependencies were added.\n\nRun it with something like `mpirun -np <ranks> ./unstructured -n 8192 -i 100`, with one rank per GPU.
